| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| "'Think as you read' questions sit inside the text at each break to check factual comprehension as the learner goes" — answering from the carrier while reading | India, Flamingo, About the Book apparatus (as filed; Hornbill's apparatus is twin) |
| The printed border between question kinds: Understanding the text (answers from it) / Talking about the text (the learner's experience and opinions) / Thinking about language | India, Hornbill and Flamingo, the unit apparatus (as filed) |
| Reading/writing strands: "facts from opinions; personal responses supported by textual evidence, viewpoints differentiated" | Ghana, NaCCA JHS curriculum, the reading/writing strands (as filed) |
| The unseen-passage comprehension of the examination years — a passage never seen before, questions answerable from it alone | Egypt, Secondary examination reading work (as filed in the book map) |
| Answering in graded shapes, one book's ladder — first, written questions on a postcard text, answers lifted from the postcard itself | China, Grade 4 Volume 2, Unit 3, Read and write, p.29 |
| The tick table: tick what you can see, checked against a four-part farm text | China, Grade 4 Volume 2, Unit 4, Read and write, p.43 |
| Read and write: read a school floor-plan text and fill the table; write school sentences (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English (PEP) Grade 4 Volume 2, Unit 1, printed p.9 |
| Read and write: Robin's e-mail advertisement "Who can be my friend?" — read it, tick what Robin can/can't do, write "I can… I can't…" sentences (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English (PEP) Grade 5 Volume 1, Unit 4, printed p.43 |
| Read and write: "Robin draws" — a park description text to tick true sentences from; write questions and answers about a nature park (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English (PEP) Grade 5 Volume 1, Unit 6, printed p.63 |
| Recycle reading set: a park-map dialogue to read and complete ("Are there any lakes in the forest?…") (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English (PEP) Grade 5 Volume 1, Recycle 2, printed pp.66–67 |
| Read and write: "Robin likes them all!" — four short season texts with photos; read and tick; write "I like … best because…" (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English (PEP) Grade 5 Volume 2, Unit 2, printed p.19 |
| Reading answered from the text: Wei Fen's story "How I Learned to Learn English" — she fell in love with Toy Story, watched faces and expressions, listened for key words, looked new words up (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English ("Go for it!") Grade 9, Unit 1, printed p.3 |
| A long dialogue text answered from the text: "Fun Times Park — Always a Fun Time!" — Alice and He Wei choose rides and ask where the restrooms are (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English ("Go for it!") Grade 9, Unit 3, printed p.19 |
| Reading: "The Difficult Search for American Products in the US" — Kang Jian in San Francisco finds toys, shoes and even American flags made in China; the reference words those/it/they must be traced (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English ("Go for it!") Grade 9, Unit 5, printed p.35 |
| Listening answered from the text: the Weifang kite festival — kites of silk or paper, an international festival every April (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English ("Go for it!") Grade 9, Unit 5, printed p.37 |
| Listening text: "The History of Potato Chips" — invented by mistake in 1853 by the cook George Crum (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English ("Go for it!") Grade 9, Unit 6, printed p.45 |
| Reading (debate article): "Should I Be Allowed to Make My Own Decisions?" — Liu Yu, a fifteen-year-old running star from Shandong, against his parents' worries (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English ("Go for it!") Grade 9, Unit 7, printed pp.54–55 |
| Reading: "What Do You Feel Like Watching Today?" — kinds of movies matched to feelings (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English ("Go for it!") Grade 9, Unit 11, printed p.67 |
| Two opinion texts set against each other: Teresa Lopez (Cali, Colombia — relaxed about time) against Marc LeBlanc (Lausanne, Switzerland — always on time) (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English ("Go for it!") Grade 9, Unit 10, printed p.75 |
| Reading: "Save the Sharks!" — a fact-sheet passage: around 70 million sharks caught and traded a year, numbers fallen by over 90 per cent in 20–30 years (stocked 2026-11-08 by the re-verification sweep) | China — English ("Go for it!") Grade 9, Unit 13, printed p.99 |
| Multiple choice: choose-the-answer questions on a packing text | China, Grade 4 Volume 2, Unit 5, Read and write, p.53 |
| The picture-only answer: advertisement questions where two answers live only in the pictured price tags, not in the text — the text alone is not enough, and the book knows it | China, Grade 4 Volume 2, Unit 6, Read and write, p.63 |
| True/false statements judged against a letter | China, Grade 4 Volume 2, Recycle 2, p.69 |
| A question-driven story text: choose the title, say who is younger, say when the shadow is longer, complete the dialogue — every answer found inside the story | China, Grade 6 Volume 2, Unit 1, Read and write, pp.8–9 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 46 countries (Egypt, Ghana,
India, China, Indonesia, Iran, Sierra Leone, Jordan, Greece, Kazakhstan, Iraq, Ethiopia, South Africa, Syria, South Sudan, Nepal, Armenia, Vietnam, Cambodia, Pakistan, Sudan, Kuwait, Rwanda, Malaysia, Yemen, Afghanistan, Ecuador, Saudi Arabia, Uzbekistan, Venezuela, Tanzania, Albania, Mexico, Mongolia, Palestine, Cyprus, Fiji, Eritrea, Tunisia, Samoa, Uganda, Uruguay, Sri Lanka, Suriname, Bhutan, Moldova).** Moldova joins with the Moldova rows stocked below (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Moldova expansion merge). Suriname joins with the Reader for Grade 10 rows stocked below (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Suriname expansion merge). Sri Lanka joins with the Sri Lanka rows stocked below (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Sri Lanka expansion merge). Uruguay joins with the #LivingUruguay rows stocked in this file (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Uruguay expansion merge). Cyprus joins with its finding-information points, from the Year 4 e-pal e-mail to the Year 6 'Away from Home' refugee readings (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Cyprus expansion merge). India adds passages set apart for answering from in Classes 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10 and 11, from "The Blue Jackal" to "Discovering Tut" (stocked 2026-11-09 by the India re-verification sweep merge). India builds the carrier hunt into the page and
prints the question-kind border; Ghana demands textual
evidence under personal responses; Egypt examines by the
unseen passage; China grades the answering shapes themselves
— postcard questions, a tick table, multiple choice, answers
that live only in the picture, true/false on a letter.
Indonesia answers a two-sided text in purpose, Yes/No,
reference and fact/opinion shapes, answers implicit
information only from evidence, and teaches answering
line-referenced questions in its Grade 10 guide; Iran
teaches scanning-for-the-answer and question generation —
making the question each main point answers; Sierra Leone
prints the factual/inferential border as an examination
system, states the questions-first routine, and faces the
unseen WASSCE text in two full lessons; Jordan adds the
true / false / doesn't-say shape with explanations at
Grade 6 and names skimming and scanning as its Grade 10
Active Reading strategy; Greece answers its long Lykeio
texts in graded shapes — true/false, short answers and
multiple choice on one feature, true/false/not-mentioned
with purpose, audience and reference questions on
another, and paragraph-location questions that send the
reader to the paragraph where the answer lives;
Kazakhstan examines by the unseen text in its Grade 9
Reading Practice — ten exam-format tasks answered from
their texts alone — and prints the third answer shape
at Grade 10, where its science book's statements are
judged True, False or Not Given;
Iraq answers from the text from primary school up —
the pond text's numbers worked in writing at Grade 3,
workbook-only texts questioned at Grade 8, an unseen
four-paragraph text examined at Grade 10, and at
Grade 12 true/false answers checked with the evidence
underlined in the text itself;
Ethiopia stocks the answering in part — its Grade 8
reading work runs on direct / inferential / referential
question triads over every text (the answer stated, the
answer deduced, the reference resolved), but no read
Ethiopia book names the honest "the text doesn't say"
answer, so that part rests on the other countries; South Africa answers in writing from the text through the whole set — its Grade 3 Home Language book's standing instruction is that answers are written in full sentences from the passage, its Grade 3 First Additional Language book answers seven written comprehension questions on a health article (printed pp.22–23), and its Grade 4 Home Language book answers comprehension and writes a 40–50-word summary of a story (Theme 1) — plainly literal-level comprehension, inference appearing only as occasional "Why do you think…?" questions; Syria answers from the text through the whole course as its standing comprehension work — its Grade 1 stories close with a tick/cross check, its Grade 3 readings are answered by multiple choice and tick/cross, and its Grade 12 scientific workbook answers a diary text True / False / Not Given, the third answer shape printed at the top of the school — with no named scanning or line-reference strategy lesson, so that naming rests on the other countries; South Sudan joined 2026-11-03: answering from the text is its books' standing comprehension work — the Primary 4 book sets comprehension on its environment passage, the Primary 5 book's fixed cycle follows every reading with comprehension, the Primary 7 literature course sets comprehension questions on setting, characters and themes on every extract, and the Primary 8 book questions its stories from the text — with no named scanning, line-reference or not-given answering strategy in any read book, so that naming rests on the other countries.
Nepal joined 2026-11-03: answering from the text is its books' standing comprehension work — the Grade 6 book's fixed unit cycle answers every reading twice over (Read and answer, True or false, Read and answer again), and every Grade 11 literature text carries an "Understanding the text" question apparatus — with no named scanning, line-reference or not-given answering strategy in any read book, so that naming rests on the other countries.
Kuwait answers from the text as its Pearls
reading pattern: Pearls 6A runs comprehension questions,
true/false with correction, and reference and inference
questions on every reading, and Pearls 9B sends pupils
to find the evidence in the text in its cause-and-effect
linker work. Rwanda answers from the text as its standing comprehension work from P1 up — passages, stories, graphs and sample papers all closed by questions on the text itself — with skimming and scanning named as reading skills in P5 and P6, but no not-given answering shape in any read book. Tanzania answers from the text as its one book's fixed cycle — every unit's story, passage or poem closed by fill-in-the-blanks, short-answer questions and a word-bank completion drawn from the text itself, two questions per unit stepping out to the pupil's own life — with no named scanning or not-given answering strategy in the book, so that naming rests on the other countries. Other closed countries join as their
books are read (see READING-QUEUE.md). Mexico answers from the text in all three grades: a physical-activity data text (Grade 7), a true/false chinampas reading and a community appeal text (Grade 8), and an identify-the-experiment reading (Grade 9) (stocked 2026-11-09 by the Mexico re-verification sweep merge). Mongolia joins with English XI and English XII, its Grade 11 and Grade 12 books, read page by page for the first time in the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Mongolia re-verification sweep merge). Eritrea joins with the *English for Eritrea* books (Grades 1–12) stocked below (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Eritrea expansion merge).

**Barbados stocked 2026-11-11 (re-verification sweep merge):** Barbados's rows in this lesson are curriculum evidence only — Barbados's set holds no pupil textbook (its held set is the four Primary Language Arts Syllabi, Classes 1–4), so the taught-in acceptance count above does not change.

**Ghana — further evidence stocked 2026-11-10 (re-verification sweep merge):** the sweep read Ghana's full Distance Learning set (six teacher guides and three learners' workbooks) and the NaCCA curriculum standards not previously cited; the new rows are teacher, curriculum and workbook evidence only — Ghana's set holds no pupil textbook, so the taught-in acceptance count above does not change.

**South Africa added 2026-11-03** — three books stock this lesson: *English Home Language, Grade 3 Workbook 1*, *English First Additional Language, Grade 3 Workbook 1* and *English Home Language, Grade 4 Workbook 1* (Rainbow Workbooks) — South Africa, Department of Basic Education, Sixteenth edition 2026; full read in english/books/south-africa.md. Comprehension questions answered in writing from the text run through the whole set: the Grade 3 Home Language book's standing instruction is that answers are written in full sentences from the passage; the Grade 3 First Additional Language book answers seven written comprehension questions on a health article (printed pp.22–23); and the Grade 4 Home Language book answers comprehension and writes a 40–50-word summary of a story (Theme 1). South Africa's comprehension is literal-level — inference appears only as occasional "Why do you think…?" questions.

**Syria added 2026-11-03** — three books stock this lesson: *Emar Pupil's Book 1*, *Emar Pupil's Book 3* and *Emar English Series 12, Workbook, Scientific Section* — Syria, Ministry of Education; full read in english/books/syria.md. Answering from the text is the course's standing comprehension work: the Grade 1 Bumbo story closes with a listen-and-tick/cross check (Stories, pp.83–88, the check on p.88); Grade 3 answers its Yamato reading by multiple-choice questions (p.47) and its dinosaurs reading by tick/cross (pp.75–77); and the Grade 12 workbook answers the diary of Diana in Montreal True / False / Not Given (Unit 10, pp.74–81). Stated plainly: Syria practises the answering shapes throughout, including the honest not-given shape, but no read Syria book names a scanning or line-reference answering strategy.

**Syria — *English for Starters 5* added 2026-11-06** — 1 further Syria book stocks this lesson: **Book:** *English for Starters 5*, Pupil's Book (pupil's book) — Syria, York Press, new edition 2008, reprinted 2014, no ISBN printed. Filed in english/books/syria.md (Grade 5 — English for Starters 5, Pupil's Book (York Press) section, FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-06). The older York Press Grade 5 course adds four citations: the Queen Zenobia reading answered with mixed wh- and yes/no questions (printed p.10); the two-text Silk Road reading with its route map (printed p.58); the Ghada Shouaa biography in three numbered parts (printed p.66); and a task type the held Syria books lack — guessing true/false with *I think / I don't think* before checking, on sun, moon and planet statements (printed p.38).

**South Sudan added 2026-11-03** — four books stock this lesson: **Book:** *Primary English 4 Pupil's Book* — South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction, © 2018; **Book:** *Primary English 5 Pupil's Book* — South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction, © 2018; **Book:** *Primary English 7 Pupil's Book* — South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction, © 2018; **Book:** *Primary English 8 Pupil's Book* — South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction, © 2018. All filed in english/books/south-sudan.md (Primary 4 — Primary English 4 Pupil's Book, Primary 5 — Primary English 5 Pupil's Book, Primary 7 — Primary English 7 Pupil's Book and Primary 8 — Primary English 8 Pupil's Book sections, FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03). Answering from the text is the standing comprehension work of the series: the Primary 4 book sets comprehension on its creation-and-environment passage (printed pp.38–39); the Primary 5 book's fixed unit cycle — vocabulary, reading text, oral discussion, comprehension — answers the road-accident narrative (printed pp.4–5) and the First World War soldier's diary (printed pp.27–29) from the texts themselves; the Primary 7 book's literature course (Units 6–10) sets comprehension questions on setting, characters and themes for every extract; and the Primary 8 book sets comprehension on its stories, among them "A sustainable future" (Unit 3, printed pp.36–47). Stated plainly: the questions are literal and literary (setting, characters, themes) — no read South Sudan book names a scanning, line-reference or not-given answering strategy.

**Nepal added 2026-11-03** — two books stock this lesson: **Book:** *English Grade 6* — Nepal, Government of Nepal, Ministry of Education, Curriculum Development Centre. **Book:** *English (Grade 11)* — Nepal, Government of Nepal, Ministry of Education, Science and Technology, Curriculum Development Centre. All filed in english/books/nepal.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03). ## Palestine — sweep additions (2026-11-11, re-verification sweep merge)

The 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep re-read all 8 of Palestine's held English books page by page — 171 teaching items per book, 164 distinct; 16 already in the course, 148 missing. The rows below are this lesson's share of the missing, stocked from the sweep's placements (liqlearns-courses-work/sweep-2026-11-08/english/palestine-placements.md). Palestine is new to this lesson: the countries line and the acceptance count above rise by one.

**Book 1:** *English for Palestine — Grade 5, Pupil's Book 5B, Blended Learning Module 4* — Palestine, Ministry of Education & Higher Education / Macmillan, 16 pp (printed page = PDF page − 1). The only Grade 5 English book in hand — no Grade 5 main book is in the set; the module is a condensed extract of main-book units, and its Unit 3 twice sends the reader to p.56 of the main book for a reading text NOT printed in the module (sweep diff, flag F7). Its errors are taught corrected: the worksheet header prints "Rivision" for Revision (three times), one passage says "arrive to Gaza" (for arrive in/at Gaza), another "science laps" (for science labs). Recorded record-only, unread in the filing; read page by page for the first time in the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 2:** *English for Palestine — Grade 10 Part 1* (Pupil's Book 10A) — Palestine, Ministry of Education & Higher Education / Macmillan, 97 pp. Recorded record-only, unread in english/books/palestine.md; read page by page for the first time in the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep (placements at unit/period grain from the book's contents and period pages).

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Reading a tour itinerary table and answering from it | Palestine, Grade 5 Blended Learning Module 4, Unit 1, printed pp.2–3 |

The rows below are this lesson's share, stocked from the expansion placements (liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-2026-11-10/english/uganda-placements.md). Stories and readings are stocked as plot summaries only. Printed page = PDF page − 6 in the five booklets.

**Book 1:** *English Home-Study Learning, Primary Five* (student's book) — Uganda, National Curriculum Development Centre (NCDC), Ministry of Education and Sports, August 2020, 60 pp. Read page by page in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Dialogue 'Kintu Gets COVID-19' — summary only: Juma tells Jessica that Kintu returned from Dubai, tested positive at Entebbe Airport and is quarantined; visitors are not allowed — read/acted dialogue with comprehension questions | Uganda — *English Home-Study Learning, Primary Five* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.11–12 |
| Poem comprehension: a short two-stanza poem about COVID-19 as 'a funny but terrible disease' (by Matovu Brian) — theme summary only — answered in full sentences (stanzas, poet, safety items) | Uganda — *English Home-Study Learning, Primary Five* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.28–29 |
| Passage comprehension — summary only: Namudira, a Ugandan girl in Butaleja District, writes about her Kenyan friend Betty and Chinese friend Lee, respecting each other's cultures and food, and her school's annual cultural festival | Uganda — *English Home-Study Learning, Primary Five* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.37–38 |
| Poem 'Languages' (by Jireh Atugonza) — summary only: different peoples speak different languages; Uganda has many languages and English brings Ugandans together — comprehension in full sentences | Uganda — *English Home-Study Learning, Primary Five* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.40–41 |

**Book 2:** *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior One* (student's book) — Uganda, National Curriculum Development Centre (NCDC), Ministry of Education and Sports, August 2020, 44 pp. Read page by page in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Reading passage 'Child Labour' — summary only: millions of children are trapped in child labour that denies health and education; about 168 million children aged 5–17 are affected, and prevention means protecting children's rights under the UN Convention on the Rights of the Child | Uganda — *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior One* (NCDC, 2020), printed p.7 |
| Reading passage 'Pollution in Uganda' — summary only: pollution is a leading killer in developing countries; in Kampala air pollution comes mainly from transport/rapid motorisation, in rural areas from firewood cooking (article by Serginho Roosblad, 2015) — comprehension plus a 3-sentence summary of the dangers | Uganda — *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior One* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.8–9 |
| Two poems compared — summaries only: 'Urban Life' (Webster Mubonwa: noise, factory pollution, rushing, paying for everything) and 'The Village' (Marma Gashe: Kanyiriri village of toil, old women trudging to weedy maize plots) — noting differences between town and village life | Uganda — *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior One* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.11–12 |

**Book 3:** *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior Three* (student's book) — Uganda, National Curriculum Development Centre (NCDC), Ministry of Education and Sports, August 2020, 56 pp. Read page by page in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Reading passage — summary only: Ms Sheila Alice Angaza becomes the first Minister for Ethics, Integrity and Transparency in the fictional Republic of Tchweza and argues the country itself needs lessons in honesty after colonialism, coups and civil wars — comprehension on honesty/dishonesty | Uganda — *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior Three* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.3–5 |
| Reading passage 'Appreciate Yourself the Way You Are' — summary only: accept your body and abilities as you are; Auma answers Ojambo's teasing about her big head and thin legs by taking pride in her brain and her legs that carry her first to school — comprehension | Uganda — *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior Three* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.6–7 |
| Reading story — summary only: 'great people never die' — Mwalimu Julius Nyerere, the teacher who led Tanganyika to uhuru out of love for his country; and Bogere, a young man who shows patriotism by cleaning up his dirty suburb Naibowa — comprehension plus finding adverbs of degree in the story | Uganda — *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior Three* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.19–21 |
| Dialogue comprehension — summary only: Rosie's family debates her poor exam results — Aunt Betty says stay at school and work for a good job, Mother says leave and marry, Joseph says her practical brains suit farm work; Rosie is not ready to marry — plus writing a composition about your own future plans | Uganda — *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior Three* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.29–31 |
| Reading passage 'Opening a Bank Account' — summary only: from hiding money in bags and holes to bank accounts; paying in with a paying-in slip and counterfoil, cheque books, bounced cheques and being overdrawn ('in red'), statements — with the bank dialogue (Nambi opens a current account) put in order (the banking-word definitions are stocked in money-and-prices) | Uganda — *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior Three* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.38–41 |

**Book 4:** *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior Four* (student's book) — Uganda, National Curriculum Development Centre (NCDC), Ministry of Education and Sports, August 2020, 34 pp. Read page by page in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Article 'Choosing a Career' — summary only: know yourself (skills, likes/dislikes, values), find out about occupations, make decisions, take action; a satisfying career needs a well-thought-out decision (its summary task is stocked in summarising) | Uganda — *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior Four* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.3–5 |
| Reading comprehension by skimming and scanning: two career portraits — summary only: Mkami, who left an accounts job to build a crafts business with her carpenter cousin Jimmy, now employing 40 people; and Asha, the office manager who 'keeps the company going' at an education-book publisher — advantages/disadvantages and advice extracted | Uganda — *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior Four* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.6–7 |
| Reading for meaning: 'What do employers expect?' — summary only: employers want school leavers who know their own minds, have done homework before the interview, are ready to learn from less-qualified experienced workers, respect colleagues and are ambitious without greed — comprehension of a job-readiness passage (its job-readiness theme is stocked in job-interviews) | Uganda — *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior Four* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.10–11 |
| Comprehension passage on globalisation — summary only: globalisation as growing interconnectedness (WHO wording quoted in the book): multinational companies, fast flows of goods, services, finance, people and ideas; an emerging global culture (baseball caps, jeans, global music/film) can clash with local traditions, and world languages like English dominate while local languages may die out; the 'global village' — with the Four Palm Hotel staff passage | Uganda — *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior Four* (NCDC, 2020), printed pp.20–21 |

Uganda is new to this lesson: the countries line and the acceptance count above were updated together.
Placements: liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-2026-11-10/english/uganda-placements.md.

## Uruguay added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Uruguay's held English set from the PDFs — ANEP's *#LivingUruguay 1–6* student books (201, 259, 270, 341, 308 and 314 pages). The records file the books as Grades 7–12; every cover numbers them 1st–6th grade of secondary school — the same books under two numbering systems (flag F1); the rows below cite the records' Grades 7–12, with the cover numbering stated in each book entry. The set is six student's books and no teacher books, so every row below rests on a student's book. 660 teaching items were placed fresh from the books; Uruguay had no presence in the course before, so all 660 were missing. The rows below are this lesson's share, stocked from the expansion placements. Read depth, as the placements declare it: full page-by-page reads of all six books from their printed text layers (printed page = PDF page in all six books). The placements exclude by name only front matter, the Spanish rubric and learning-progression apparatus at each book's end, and Book 4's Spanish appendix article (p.332) and colouring page (p.333); none of it is stocked. Stories and readings are stocked as plot summaries only.

**Book 1:** *#LivingUruguay 1* (student's book) — Uruguay, ANEP (Administración Nacional de Educación Pública). Cover: “1ST GRADE ENGLISH BOOK” — the 1st grade of secondary school; filed in the records as Grade 7. Printed page = PDF page. Read in full, page by page, in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 2:** *#LivingUruguay 2* (student's book) — Uruguay, ANEP (Administración Nacional de Educación Pública). Cover: “2ND GRADE ENGLISH BOOK” — the 2nd grade of secondary school; filed in the records as Grade 8. Printed page = PDF page. Read in full, page by page, in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 3:** *#LivingUruguay 3* (student's book) — Uruguay, ANEP (Administración Nacional de Educación Pública). Cover: “3RD GRADE ENGLISH BOOK” — the 3rd grade of secondary school; filed in the records as Grade 9. Printed page = PDF page. Read in full, page by page, in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 4:** *#LivingUruguay 4* (student's book) — Uruguay, ANEP (Administración Nacional de Educación Pública). Cover: “4TH GRADE ENGLISH BOOK” — the 4th grade of secondary school; filed in the records as Grade 10. Printed page = PDF page. Read in full, page by page, in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 5:** *#LivingUruguay 5* (student's book) — Uruguay, ANEP (Administración Nacional de Educación Pública). Cover: “5TH GRADE ENGLISH BOOK” — the 5th grade of secondary school; filed in the records as Grade 11. Printed page = PDF page. Read in full, page by page, in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 6:** *#LivingUruguay 6* (student's book) — Uruguay, ANEP (Administración Nacional de Educación Pública). Cover: “6TH GRADE ENGLISH BOOK” — the 6th grade of secondary school; filed in the records as Grade 12. Printed page = PDF page. Read in full, page by page, in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's in-hand English set from the PDFs: **25 distinct documents** (31 record files; 6 byte-identical duplicate files collapsed with hash proof — the Grade 8 Teacher's Guide 2017 filed under three language names is one English-medium guide, the Grade 12 and Grade 13 A/L English and General English guides are single two-grade guides filed twice each, the Grade 7 English Language Guide 2007 'Tamil' file is the English file, and the ABOE 2018 Key Stage 1 handbook is one book for Grades 1 and 2 filed under both grades). The union set is: 3 EPD pupil's books (Grades 6, 8, 9), 4 EPD workbooks (Grades 6–9), 17 NIE teacher guides and manuals, the 2 old ABOE teacher guides plus the ABOE 2018 Key Stage 1 handbook, and the O/L *Appreciation of English Literary Texts* syllabus bound with its Teacher's Resource Book. No Grade 7 pupil's book exists in any record — Grade 7 rests on its workbook and teacher guides (flag F2). 250 teaching items were placed fresh from the books; Sri Lanka had no presence in the course before, so all 250 were missing. Evidence kinds are labelled in every book entry below: pupil's-book and workbook rows rest on pupil-side books; teacher-guide rows are teacher-guide evidence of the taught syllabus (the taught content of the NIE guides IS their syllabus/competency tables). Read depth, as the placements declare it: full page-by-page text-layer reads of the ABOE guides and handbook, the Grade 3, 5, 7 (2007 syllabus), 9, A/L and Appreciation books; the NIE secondary guides (Grades 6, 7 (2016), 8, 10, 11) read through their complete syllabus/competency tables plus the guide bodies' competency chapters; the EPD pupil books and workbooks read at full unit level with grammar sections verified line-level — except the Grade 6 Workbook, an image-only scan read from rendered pages (flag F9). The placements' named exclusions (front matter and boilerplate, assessment and pedagogy apparatus, the ABOE handbook's Sinhala-medium sections) stand as declared and are stocked nowhere. Stories and prescribed literary texts are stocked as titles and plot summaries only. The rows below are this lesson's share, stocked from the expansion placements.

**Book 1:** *English Language Teacher's Guide*, Grade 5 — National Institute of Education (NIE), First Edition 2020 — Sri Lanka. NIE syllabus (competency framework) plus teacher guide; teacher-guide evidence of the taught Grade 5 syllabus. Read in full in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 2:** *English Language Teacher's Guide*, Grade 6 — National Institute of Education (NIE), First Edition 2014 (syllabus for 2015) — Sri Lanka. NIE syllabus (47 competency levels) plus guide; teacher-guide evidence. Its syllabus table is printed pp.15–42. Read in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 3:** *English Teacher's Guide*, Grade 7 — National Institute of Education (NIE), First Edition 2016 — Sri Lanka. NIE syllabus (38 competency levels) plus guide; teacher-guide evidence. Its appendix prints the Grade 7 textbook's listening texts (the Grade 7 pupil's book itself is not in hand — flag F2). Read in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 4:** *English Language Teacher's Guide*, Grade 7 — National Institute of Education (NIE), 2007 syllabus edition — Sri Lanka. Syllabus plus fully worked lesson sequences carrying the actual pupil texts and dialogues; teacher-guide evidence, a different edition from the 2016 Grade 7 guide. (The record's 'Tamil' file is this same English file — one blob, cited once; flag F5.) Read in full in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 5:** *English Workbook*, Grade 7 — Educational Publications Department (EPD), First Print 2015 — Sri Lanka. A pupil workbook; no Grade 7 pupil's textbook is in hand in any record (flag F2), so this workbook — whose units mirror the textbook's — is the pupil-side evidence for Grade 7. Read at full unit level in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 6:** *English Workbook*, Grade 8 — Educational Publications Department (EPD), First Print 2016 — Sri Lanka. A pupil workbook whose units mirror the Grade 8 Pupil's Book; read at full unit level in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Reading aloud words/phrases/sentences in short familiar texts; understanding frequent vocabulary; extracting specific information from simple texts with pictures; reading for pleasure | Sri Lanka, English Language Teacher's Guide, Grade 5 (NIE, 2020), pp.27–28, 48–55 |
| Reading skills: using picture/visual clues; extracting specific information (names, dates, days, places) with wh-questions; transferring information into other forms; extracting the general idea of a text | Sri Lanka, English Language Teacher's Guide, Grade 6 (NIE, 2014), pp.23–26 |

Record: grade-12/approved-private/nigeria/english-teacher-guide.pdf (817,958 bytes; the read copy is byte-identical to the record).

| Passage "Writing English composition": a composition is related paragraphs written for a course or examination; the steps — generate a topic, generate ideas, select the relevant ones, arrange them, develop them into connected paragraphs | Nigeria — New Concept English Teacher's Guide 2 (Grade 11 / SSS2, Learn Africa) — teacher evidence, printed p.27 |
| Passage "Animals plan to overthrow man": Old Major's dream of an earth without man; his advice and resolutions — animals must not live in a house, drink alcohol, or copy the evils of man; read for critical evaluation | Nigeria — New Concept English Teacher's Guide 2 (Grade 11 / SSS2, Learn Africa) — teacher evidence, printed pp.31–32 |
| Passage "The joy of victory": Macumazahn and Umslopogaas watch a battle, plan to kill Agon, and travel by night through empty villages | Nigeria — New Concept English Teacher's Guide 2 (Grade 11 / SSS2, Learn Africa) — teacher evidence, printed p.34 |
| Passage "Graduation speech": a distance-education graduation — students overcame the constraints of distance, work, family and social demands; graduates gained employment, promotion and job retention from their certificates | Nigeria — New Concept English Teacher's Guide 2 (Grade 11 / SSS2, Learn Africa) — teacher evidence, printed p.38 |
| Passage "Stanley meets Mutesa": the meeting of the explorer and the king; the carriers' hardships — heat by day, cold and mosquitoes by night, loads falling from battered chests | Nigeria — New Concept English Teacher's Guide 2 (Grade 11 / SSS2, Learn Africa) — teacher evidence, printed p.41 |
| Passage "Bad conversation habits": not listening, impatience and interrupting, dominating a conversation; maintaining eye contact and reading body language | Nigeria — New Concept English Teacher's Guide 2 (Grade 11 / SSS2, Learn Africa) — teacher evidence, printed p.44 |
| Passage "The destructive power of anger": anger is antagonism toward someone felt to have done wrong; it contributes to heart disease; managing anger — notice it, know its triggers, begin the day with quiet time, be at peace with yourself and others | Nigeria — New Concept English Teacher's Guide 2 (Grade 11 / SSS2, Learn Africa) — teacher evidence, printed pp.48–49 |

Nigeria enters this lesson as teacher evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — Nigeria's in-hand set is teacher guides only, cited as the evidence kind they are, never counted as a pupil's book (the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent).

## Zimbabwe added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Zimbabwe's in-hand English set from the PDFs — **7 teacher's guides, 0 student books**: the PlusOne English Teacher's Guides for ECD A and ECD B and Grades 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 (Secondary Book Press, Harare, a MoPSE-approved publisher) — 755 teaching items placed. Every file was retrieved from the textbooks repo (three by the tree route, four as release assets) and verified against its staging record by exact byte size, page count and SHA-256. Every Zimbabwe item in this section is TEACHER EVIDENCE of what is taught: Zimbabwe has no student English book in hand, and no row below is pupil-book evidence; a teacher's guide never substitutes for a pupil's book. Read depth, as the placements declare it: all seven guides read in full, page by page through their text layers (pdftotext, every page); the matching Learner's Books and Workbooks are not in hand and were not read — their content is evidenced only through the guides. The rows below are stocked from the expansion placements (zimbabwe-placements.md); where the placements name the same item for a second lesson, the row appears in that lesson too.

**Book 1:** *PlusOne English Grade 3 Teacher's Guide* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a teacher's guide, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Zimbabwe, Secondary Book Press, Harare (first published 2025), ISBN 978-1-77932-042-1, 115 pp. Printed page = the guide's printed footer page (printed p.1 = PDF p.16); pages cited are printed pages.

**Book 2:** *PlusOne English Grade 4 Teacher's Guide* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a teacher's guide, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Zimbabwe, Secondary Book Press, Harare (first published 2026), ISBN 978-1-77932-041-4, 140 pp. Printed page = the guide's printed footer page (printed p.1 = PDF p.16); pages cited are printed pages.

**Book 3:** *PlusOne English Grade 5 Teacher's Guide* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a teacher's guide, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Zimbabwe, Secondary Book Press, Harare (first published 2026), ISBN 978-1-77932-040-7, 104 pp. Printed page = the guide's printed footer page (printed p.1 = PDF p.16); pages cited are printed pages.

**Book 4:** *PlusOne English Grade 6 Teacher's Guide* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a teacher's guide, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Zimbabwe, Secondary Book Press, Harare (first published 2026), ISBN 978-1-77932-039-1, 150 pp. Printed page = the guide's printed footer page (printed p.1 = PDF p.16); pages cited are printed pages.

**Book 5:** *PlusOne English Grade 7 Teacher's Guide* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a teacher's guide, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Zimbabwe, Secondary Book Press, Harare (first published 2026), ISBN 978-1-77932-038-4, 145 pp. Printed page = the guide's printed footer page (printed p.1 = PDF p.16); pages cited are printed pages.

## Canada added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Canada's in-hand English set from the PDFs — **2 teacher resource guides, 0 student books**: *English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide* (FNESC/FNSA, 2018; 409 pp) and *English 12 First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide* (FNESC, 2008; 307 PDF pages), the guides for British Columbia's English First Peoples courses — 145 teaching items placed. Both files were grabbed through the authenticated GitHub route and verified byte-exact against the library record (2018 guide 5,438,437 bytes; 2008 guide 3,393,665 bytes). Every Canada item in this section is TEACHER EVIDENCE of what the BC English First Peoples courses teach: Canada has no student English book in hand, and no row below is pupil-book evidence; a teacher's guide never substitutes for a pupil's book. Read depth, as the placements declare it: both guides read in full, page by page through their text layers (716 PDF pages); the 2018 guide amalgamates and replaces the 2008 guide, so where the two carry the same teaching, each is cited on its own evidence. The rows below are stocked from the expansion placements (canada-placements.md); where the placements name the same item for a second lesson, the row appears in that lesson too.

**Book 1:** *English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a teacher resource guide, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Canada (British Columbia), First Nations Education Steering Committee and First Nations Schools Association (FNESC/FNSA), West Vancouver, ©2018, 409 pp. Printed page = PDF page (the footer numbers match the PDF pages throughout); pages cited are printed pages.

**Book 2:** *English 12 First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a teacher resource guide, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Canada (British Columbia), First Nations Education Steering Committee (FNESC), ©2008, 307 PDF pages. Printed page = PDF page − 1 (the Table of Contents is printed p.7 = PDF p.8); pages cited are printed pages.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Childhood through the Eyes of Indigenous Writers, Grade 10: reading children's picture books as texts — how words and pictures together carry perspectives on children, beliefs and values; gallery-walk reading with group roles (reader, discussion manager, recorder, reporter) | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.85–88 |
| How Do We Define Ourselves?: identity in poetry — Rita Joe's “Who are you?” and “I Lost My Talk” read as identity poems (named poems, studied in the guide, not reprinted there) | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.197–204 |
| Belonging, Grade 10: character study with textual proof — a visual “three flower bouquet” for a character, properly quoting the text as evidence (the character-study part is also stocked in story-techniques) | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.226–228 |
| Relationships: literature-circle quotation response — choosing pertinent quotations from your novel section and responding to each: why it matters, and a question it raises | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.293–298 |
| Lost People: the lost-people theme in poetry — Connie Fife's “Communications Class” and Duncan Mercredi's “racing across the land” read and discussed as poems | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.320–322 |
| The Trickster — A Hard Character to Pin Down: the Trickster archetype traced through short stories, poetry and essays, by the jigsaw discussion method | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.378–382 |
| Unit 2: The Oral Tradition: the printed response-question set on Thomas King's chapter “'You'll Never Believe What Happened' Is Always a Great Way to Start” | Canada — English 12 First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC, 2008) — teacher evidence, printed pp.45–46 |
| Unit 4: Lost People: poetry response with its own scoring guide — responding in writing to Connie Fife's “Communications Class” or Duncan Mercredi's “racing across the land”, and comparing “His Kitchen” with “Blue Against White” (later form: the 2018 guide's Lost People poetry row above) | Canada — English 12 First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC, 2008) — teacher evidence, printed pp.68–72 |
| Unit 5: Residential Schooling: Rita Joe's “I Lost My Talk”, reproduced in the guide and studied in full — filed as summary only: the speaker's Mi'kmaq talk was snatched away at the Shubenacadie residential school; the poem's broken form (sentence fragments punctuated as sentences, unpunctuated clauses, the “scrambled ballad” line) mirrors its theme, and the tone shifts from accusation to a gentle closing plea (the form-mirrors-theme part is also stocked in poetry-writing) | Canada — English 12 First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC, 2008) — teacher evidence, printed pp.95–97 |
| Unit 6: From the Heart — Poetry: Duncan Mercredi's “Morning Awakening” (from *Spirit of the Wolf*), reproduced in the unit's poem materials and studied there | Canada — English 12 First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC, 2008) — teacher evidence, printed pp.101–108 |
| Unit 12: Métis Literature: poetry study — the Métis poets of the unit's Part IV poetry section | Canada — English 12 First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC, 2008) — teacher evidence, printed pp.271–282 |

Book error carried, taught corrected: Book 2's Unit 5 journal prompt at printed p.95 is garbled in the book itself (“Why is think Mariesis is worried about Champion going off to school?”) — noted here and never modelled; no row above rests on the prompt.

## Singapore added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)


The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Singapore's in-hand English set from the PDFs — **2 teacher files, 0 student books**: the *NEL Educators' Guide for Language and Literacy (English Language)* (Ministry of Education, Singapore, 2022 series ©2023; 125 pp) and the *Big Book Teaching Steps* sheet (2 pp) — the preschool educator guides for Nursery 2 – Kindergarten 2 (ages 4–6) — 72 teaching items placed. Both files were grabbed from the official MOE source named in the record and verified byte-exact AND hash-exact against the staging books.json record before reading (the guide: 2,644,142 bytes, SHA-256 9471e9f051216641f13268f5b8008dfa13183ad8c1041be2b47996de31cbfb59; the sheet: 172,494 bytes, SHA-256 ed83b68e248f95a62df54b6da0ea74a66d7e4238fb979e61177657f149bf3bc6). Every Singapore item in this section is TEACHER EVIDENCE of what is taught: Singapore has no student English book in hand, and no row below is pupil-book evidence; a teacher's guide never substitutes for a pupil's book. Read depth, as the placements declare it: both files read in full, page by page through their text layers; no reduced-depth read. The rows below are stocked from the expansion placements (singapore-placements.md); where the placements name the same item for a second lesson, the row appears in that lesson too.

**Book 1:** *NEL Educators' Guide for Language and Literacy (English Language)* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a preschool educator's guide, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Singapore, Ministry of Education, Republic of Singapore (2022 series, ©2023), ISBN 978-981-18-7427-7, 125 pp, for Nursery 2 – Kindergarten 2 (ages 4–6). Printed page = PDF page through the body (each body page is headed with its printed number; front matter PDF pp.1–8 is unnumbered apparatus); pages cited are printed pages.

**Book 2:** *Big Book Teaching Steps* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a 2-page procedures sheet for the Shared Book Approach, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book — the NEL Big Books themselves are print-only classroom storybooks and are not in hand; this sheet is the only Big-Book PDF the portal publishes) — Singapore, Ministry of Education, 2 pp. No printed page numbers; cited as pp.1–2.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|

| Comprehension skills as a named set (KSD 3.6–3.6.4): talking and asking questions about a story; talking about characters and events; responding to literal and inferential questions; retelling and sequencing events (including by dramatising); predicting from the cover and predicting what happens next; suggesting a different ending; constructing meaning from texts in other media | Singapore — NEL Educators' Guide for Language and Literacy (English Language) (MOE, 2023) — teacher evidence, printed pp.41, 46 |

| Dialogic reading: the child moves from listener to storyteller through the five CROWD prompt types — Completion (supply the missing word), Recall (events and characters), Open-ended (talk about a picture), Wh- questions on the pictures, Distancing (relate the story to your own experience) | Singapore — NEL Educators' Guide for Language and Literacy (English Language) (MOE, 2023) — teacher evidence, printed pp.68–69 |

| The prediction and responding strands of the five-step Shared Book Approach: prediction questions at the cover and during the first reading, illustration questions, and children responding after the straight-through second reading (e.g. drawing their favourite characters) (the full five-step procedure is stocked in the-big-reading) | Singapore — Big Book Teaching Steps (MOE) — teacher evidence, pp.1–2 |

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's in-hand English set from the PDFs — **2 teacher programmes, 0 student books**: the *Programa de llengua anglesa de segona ensenyança de l'Escola Andorrana* (Decret 361/2023, BOPA Núm. 91, 19 July 2023; 76 BOPA pages) and the *Programa de comunicació en llengua anglesa del batxillerat general de l'Escola Andorrana* (Decret del 20-3-2019, BOPA Núm. 29, 27 March 2019; 40 BOPA pages) — the Escola Andorrana's official English curriculum programmes, decreed in Catalan — 70 teaching items placed. Both programmes were read from the BOPA originals the records name (the GitHub contents route failed on both files — connection closed / empty reply, tried twice by two clients — declared in the placements): the Batxillerat file matches its staging record byte-exactly (890,148 bytes, 40 pages; SHA-256 9f8a16cff149e7890b2e185318855a9ebca98653cb97610dc9eece20b5990c61), and the Segona file (28,016,485 bytes, 76 pages; SHA-256 139b15162d27739df0defc55cf3dc1e7968e1d5b00dc0a8737e9d36734293a07) is identity-verified by the Decret 361/2023 header on its PDF p.1, no record carrying a byte size for it. Every Andorra item in this section is TEACHER EVIDENCE of what Andorra's English programmes teach: Andorra has no student English book in hand, and no row below is pupil-book evidence; a teacher programme never substitutes for a pupil's book. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Segona programme's five Recursos blocs (BOPA pp.34–63) were read page by page in full; its competence chapters, didactic orientations and synthesis tables were read structurally and excluded as apparatus restating the blocs. The Batxillerat programme's three competence chapters and the three Recursos bloc openings were read; its criteria, expectations and synthesis tables were read structurally and excluded as apparatus; some of its body pages extract with a substituted-glyph text layer, decoded via the consistent substitution and cross-checked against the same pages' clean-font headings and lists. The rows below are stocked from the expansion placements (andorra-placements.md); where the placements name the same item for a second lesson, the row appears in that lesson too.

**Book 1:** *Programa de llengua anglesa de segona ensenyança de l'Escola Andorrana* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a curriculum programme decreed by the Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Decret 361/2023 (BOPA Núm. 91, 19 July 2023), 76 pp, for Segona ensenyança years 1–4 (two cycles). Pages cited are the BOPA printed pages (shown on every page as N/76; the PDF page number is the same number).

**Book 2:** *Programa de comunicació en llengua anglesa del batxillerat general de l'Escola Andorrana* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a curriculum programme decreed by the Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Decret del 20-3-2019 (BOPA Núm. 29, 27 March 2019), 40 pp, for Batxillerat General years 1–2. Pages cited are the BOPA printed pages (N/40 = the PDF page number).

## Zambia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Zambia's in-hand English set from the PDFs — **12 documents, all teacher-facing: eleven Curriculum Development Centre teaching modules and the Catch Up Literacy Teacher's Guide; 0 pupil's books** — 164 teaching items placed. Every file was secured from its recorded original source (the CDC Digital Library; the Catch Up guide from its recorded VVOB source) at a byte size matching the textbooks repo's contents record exactly, and verified by byte size, page count and cover. Every Zambia item in this section is TEACHER EVIDENCE of what is taught: Zambia has no pupil English book in hand — five of the twelve files are even recorded as “student” books in the records, but their own covers say TEACHING MODULE (expansion flags F2/F3) — and no row below is pupil-book evidence; a teaching module never substitutes for a pupil's book. Two of the modules state why the set looks this way: they were produced “in the absence of approved textbooks.” Read depth, as the placements declare it: all 12 books read in full, page by page through their text layers (the two Grade 1 phonics modules run one seven-part lesson shape per sound — every subtopic's overview and competences were read and the full shape verified in complete reads of multiple subtopics per book; individual exercise lines inside a sound subtopic are practice of the placed sound and are not itemised). The rows below are stocked from the expansion placements (zambia-placements.md); where the placements name the same item for a second lesson, the row appears in that lesson too.

**Book 1:** *Literacy Teacher's Guide — Catch Up Programme (Grades 3–5)* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a teacher's guide, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Zambia, Ministry of Education with Pratham, ZESSTA, VVOB, TaRL Africa, UNICEF and USAID, 2023. 35 pp; 7,033,604 bytes, SHA-256 a34cdc66…; grabbed from its recorded VVOB source at exactly its recorded size. Pages cited are the book's printed folios. Language caveat (expansion flag F6): the guide is written in English but directs the teacher to teach in the learners' familiar (Zambian) language and translate all examples, and its sample assessment tool is printed in Cinyanja — its rows are early-literacy SKILLS evidence for Grades 3–5, not English-medium pupil content.

**Book 5:** *Literacy in English Language Teaching Module, Grade 2 Term 3* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a teaching module, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Zambia, Curriculum Development Centre, Lusaka, 2026. 78 pp; 1,151,721 bytes, SHA-256 c99d9326…; CDC Digital Library download byte-matching the textbooks repo's contents record.
